\title{Literature Review Report}
\date{2026-08-23}

\begin{document}
\maketitle

\begin{abstract}
Research question: (Q1) Which aspects of preparing documents with LaTeX -- the compile model (source text is compiled into a separate typeset output; what you see is not what you edit; the output only changes on recompilation; whitespace and special characters in the source have meaning), error handling (reading TeX/compiler error messages as information naming a place and a cause; fixing the first error first; the edit-compile loop as a normal working mode), and semantic markup (declaring what something is -- \section, \emph, \ref, \cite -- rather than how it looks; the same source rendering differently under another class or style; cross-references and numbering maintained by the compiler) -- does empirical literature identify as ones learners must discern or repeatedly fail to? Adjacent literatures count: HTML/CSS and markup-language education (novices' markup errors, structure versus presentation, separation of content and style), WYSIWYG-versus-markup or word-processor-styles user studies, compiler-error-message reading by novices, end-user programming of documents, structured-document authoring. (Q2) Has learners' understanding of LaTeX, document preparation or markup languages been MEASURED -- by a phenomenographic/interview study, a diagnostic instrument or concept inventory, a knowledge test, or a pre/post-test? Also relevant: method precedents -- development/validation of two-tier diagnostic tests and concept inventories in computing, and learning-study pre/post-test designs on critical aspects (variation theory, Marton, Pang). KEEP a paper if it reports empirical data on learners' or users' difficulties, errors, misconceptions, conceptions or understanding of LaTeX/TeX, markup languages (HTML, CSS, XML, Markdown, wiki markup), WYSIWYG vs markup editing, word-processor structure/styles, or compiler/validator error-message reading; or if it measures such understanding with an instrument; or if it is a method precedent (concept inventory / diagnostic test development in computing, learning-study pre/post designs). DISCARD papers about: rubber/latex materials, medicine, chemistry; accessibility tooling for equations; LaTeX as a tool for other purposes (exam generation, slides) without learner data; science-education diagnostic tests in physics/chemistry/biology (unless a general methodological review); general programming-error studies that do not concern error-message reading; unrelated fields. The search covered scopus and yielded 772 papers (33 kept, 739 discarded, 0 pending).
\end{abstract}
\clearpage
\clearpage
\tableofcontents
\clearpage

\section{Research Context}
Research question: (Q1) Which aspects of preparing documents with LaTeX
-- the compile model (source text is compiled into a separate typeset
output; what you see is not what you edit; the output only changes on
recompilation; whitespace and special characters in the source have
meaning), error handling (reading TeX/compiler error messages as
information naming a place and a cause; fixing the first error first;
the edit-compile loop as a normal working mode), and semantic markup
(declaring what something is -- \textbackslash section, \emph, \ref,
\textbackslash cite -- rather than how it looks; the same source
rendering differently under another class or style; cross-references and
numbering maintained by the compiler) -- does empirical literature
identify as ones learners must discern or repeatedly fail to? Adjacent
literatures count: HTML/CSS and markup-language education (novices'
markup errors, structure versus presentation, separation of content and
style), WYSIWYG-versus-markup or word-processor-styles user studies,
compiler-error-message reading by novices, end-user programming of
documents, structured-document authoring. (Q2) Has learners'
understanding of LaTeX, document preparation or markup languages been
MEASURED -- by a phenomenographic/interview study, a diagnostic
instrument or concept inventory, a knowledge test, or a pre/post-test?
Also relevant: method precedents -- development/validation of two-tier
diagnostic tests and concept inventories in computing, and
learning-study pre/post-test designs on critical aspects (variation
theory, Marton, Pang). KEEP a paper if it reports empirical data on
learners' or users' difficulties, errors, misconceptions, conceptions or
understanding of LaTeX/TeX, markup languages (HTML, CSS, XML, Markdown,
wiki markup), WYSIWYG vs markup editing, word-processor
structure/styles, or compiler/validator error-message reading; or if it
measures such understanding with an instrument; or if it is a method
precedent (concept inventory / diagnostic test development in computing,
learning-study pre/post designs). DISCARD papers about: rubber/latex
materials, medicine, chemistry; accessibility tooling for equations;
LaTeX as a tool for other purposes (exam generation, slides) without
learner data; science-education diagnostic tests in
physics/chemistry/biology (unless a general methodological review);
general programming-error studies that do not concern error-message
reading; unrelated fields.
